Elemental Royalty (ELE) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Orion deal and divestiture reshape Elemental's cost base and outlook.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Elemental Royalty Corporation (ELE -4.66%) reported record year-to-date performance while announcing a strategic transition toward a pure-play royalty and streaming model. Management confirmed the acquisition of five precious metal streams and royalties from Orion Mine Finance for $290 million and the divestiture of its royalty generation business. This reorganization is intended to reduce general and administrative expenses by more than 25% and simplify the corporate structure. The company reported record revenue and gold equivalent ounces for the first nine months of 2026, supported by organic growth and contributions from the recent merger with EMX. Management stated that the expanded portfolio provides a pathway to exceed 50,000 gold equivalent ounces by 2030 from existing assets.

Frederick Augustus Ronald Peter Bell: Good afternoon, and good morning, everyone, and welcome to Elemental's Q3 2026 Earnings Presentation. And with me today, I have my colleague, Stefan Wenger, our CFO; and you have myself, Frederick Bell, CEO, to talk you through the slides. In terms of an overview of the portfolio, just to give you a summary at the beginning, the company has had a record year-to-date performance in terms of both revenue and in gold equivalent ounces.

We are -- we have the strongest growth profile from this out to 2030 that we've had in the history of the company, getting to a total from existing producing and development assets of over 50,000 gold equivalent ounces from where we are today and that is already within the portfolio [ depth ]. The portfolio remains precious metals weighted. So that is approximately 2/3 precious gold and silver and 1/3 other commodities, primarily copper. It is diversified across operator counterparty and jurisdiction and we have continued to add to that and further diversify with the most recent transaction we have announced. We also just recently closed on an upsized credit facility.

That is the second time we have upsized our credit facility this year, which means that we were both able to use that for the Orion transaction, but also maintaining financial flexibility and drawing on it going forward. In terms of capital allocation, we have a normal course issuer bid put in place during the year that we utilized particularly over the summer where the share price was low and it gives us that flexibility going forward to use it again at the same time that we have had our first dividend in Q1 and now paid on a quarterly basis going forward.

In terms of the management team, we have strengthened that as well over the course of the year, both on the finance team, on the legal team and adding to our technical team as well as we go forward. And lastly, we have -- after the close of -- the final close of the Orion transaction, we have Tether remaining a cornerstone shareholder of about 27% currently and very supportive of the company's strategy going forward.

So a highlight then in Q3 specifically and it was a very active quarter for us, both in terms of transactions that we announced and completed as well as the continuing improvement to liquidity and the capital markets profile of the company that we have done. And one particular, I think, feature was the sale of the generation unit. And what we're doing here is we are both improving that liquidity for shareholders and it came with the GDXJ inclusion for the first time in September.

And then I think the combination of closing the Vizsla transaction previously announced that we finally completed in September, alongside the acquisition of a large portfolio of 5 producing streams and royalties from Orion and the sale of our generation unit that really simplifies, streamlines the company, reduces our G&A going forward and combined with the increase in revenue from the producing assets within the Orion portfolio and that reduction in G&A going into 2027, it will drive improved free cash flow and better margins going forward into 2027.

So we're really in that quarter, continuing to build out the portfolio, both producing and key development assets in Ruby Hill with i-80 at the same time that we are streamlining the business and reducing G&A going forward. In terms of year-to-date then, what we have done, I think here, it's just under $70 million in revenue for the year-to-date. We have approximately $23 million cash on the balance sheet. We still have ability to draw on our credit facility alongside that. And in terms of gold equivalent ounces, that is just under 15,100 gold equivalent ounces year-to-date, which is a record for the company, both in terms of revenue and gold equivalent ounces.

And I think that importantly, we see really strong growth going ahead from our portfolio as it was and with the addition of the Orion portfolio where we will get some partial contribution for this year and a full year's contribution going into next year alongside Endura's asset in New Zealand that will be in production, expect to have first gold in December. So I won't go through all of these milestones here, but I think we wanted just to highlight how active the company has been over the course of this year, both on transactions and on the corporate front. And I think that Q3 has really been a continuation of that.

And we announced in -- post quarter end, we announced the first close on the Orion portfolio and we're expecting to further progress Carlin East to sell the generation business and have an announcement on that in Q4, along with the final close on the last asset in the Orion portfolio in Q1 2027. In terms of our growth outlook moving forward and I think we just highlighted this earlier, but it is really the -- by far and away, the strongest growth profile that we've ever had in the company.

And you can see what it was prior to the Orion acquisition and then in the silver there, the addition of that portfolio, which is a partial year contribution this year in 2026 and then a full year in 2027 onwards and really wraps up with the contribution from i-80's Ruby Hill, where we have a very material silver stream there. And I think what you will see going into 2027 is you will see alongside that increase in gold equivalent ounces and revenue, you will also see a step down in terms of our G&A and costs that Stefan can expand on a bit further in this call. And that is driven by the sale of our generation unit.

And just as a reminder for everyone, in terms of NAV, that business unit probably represented order of magnitude, 1% or less of our NAV, but it was 60% of the personnel in the company. It was a material number of subsidiaries and in excess of 25% of our G&A as well. And so by doing this transaction and did happen simultaneously. But by doing it simultaneously, we're expanding our revenue, reducing our costs and again, should be improving that free cash flow margin going into 2027. And -- so with that, I will hand over to Stefan to run through the financial highlights for the quarter.

And then Stefan will hand back to me a bit later on to talk to a few of the asset-specific pieces and happy to take Q&A at the end of this call.

Stefan Wenger: Thank you, Fred, and good morning, everyone. Thanks for participating in our quarterly conference call. As you can -- as Fred mentioned, it was a really active quarter. The team is really performing well and we are executing on our strategy for the year. A couple of financial highlights. You can see on a revenue basis and an adjusted EBITDA basis, our results, both for Q3 and year-to-date show a substantial increase compared to the prior year. As you know, in the prior year, we were -- we have not yet merged with EMX. So this really shows the benefit of the merger.

In addition to the strong performance of our assets throughout the year, this is driven by GEOs of about 15,000 year-to-date. And you can see in the bottom there that our guidance, we're well progressing towards our full year guidance that we updated in Q3 with the announcement of Orion. So we're operating very well on all fronts on the financial perspective. As I dig into the financials a little bit more, you can see our revenue is up by 153%. Our adjusted EBITDA is up by close to 170% and we have cash of $23 million at the end of the year.

Thinking about the income statement, the question I get most is what do we expect the G&A to look like for the full year and then going forward. And this year has really been a transitional year. We've experienced higher-than-expected G&A because of our integration costs between Elemental and EMX, but also now we're -- we've spent a significant amount of time and effort in the past quarter with the divestiture of the generative business that Fred mentioned. And as we head into 2027, we're going to see the G&A decrease.

This quarter, we had higher G&A costs specifically because of some severance costs related to the divestment of the generative business and also legal costs as we work to complete that in the fourth quarter. I would expect the fourth quarter costs are still going to be in line with the third quarter. But as we head into 2027, we're going to start to really see the benefits. We're going to see the benefits in a couple of ways. Number one, the royalty generation expenses that you see there that are about $5 million a year are going to go away.

And in addition to that, because of the reduction in head count that Fred mentioned and also streamlining activities that we have within the company for simplification of our business on top of the divestiture of the German business, we're going to see significant savings on G&A. We quoted in our release when we announced the Orion acquisition that our G&A reduction -- or our cost reduction will be $6 million a year. And with those additional simplification efforts that we see, we see that we're going to save well beyond $6 million a year and we'll update that as we enter 2027. From a capital structure perspective, it was also a transitional quarter with respect to our capital structure.

We not only completed the acquisition of Vizsla, but we also -- which we drew $30 million on our line of credit. We also positioned ourselves to go ahead and acquire the Orion portfolio. So we've expanded our credit facility. And as of today, we do have $210 million of debt outstanding and $23 million of cash plus equity investments of about $36 million. So on a debt-to-EBITDA basis, we're about 2.5x net debt to EBITDA, but we see that deleveraging rather quickly. And over the next year, you'll see that nearly have as we go forward. So we see that we have still good availability with the credit facility and the accordion feature of about $90 million.

And as was mentioned, with increasing cash flow from the Orion deal, which for next year is about $20 million at current prices plus the reduction of our G&A in the generative business, we're going to be expanding our cash flow significantly that will support that debt reduction. Just a little more detail on the cash flow. For Q3, you can see we started the quarter with $74 million in cash. We generated $13 million of free cash flow just from our royalties and then invested $53.5 million for the Vizsla acquisition, another $25 million for the addition and enhancement of our investment in Quilla and the Chapi project.

And we drew on the line to fund some of those activities, ending the quarter with $23 million in cash. Just for the full year, you can see all of the activities that Fred outlined. And in addition to what I discussed on Vizsla and Chapi, we had other royalty acquisitions during the period and the draw on the RCF. You can see also we continue to fund our dividend. The dividend, we anticipate continuing to fund that and increase that into the future. And we also bought back some shares when we were in the lower share prices back in June and July. We were active on our NCIB as well.

So you can see quite active on the capital front. And with that, I'm going to turn it back to Fred for a portfolio update and then we'll take questions.

Frederick Augustus Ronald Peter Bell: Thank you, Stefan. And looking here at the global map of our portfolio, I think what stands out is we are really cornerstone now by a number of both international miners such as Lundin, Zijin, but also some Nevada Gold Mines, some specific companies in Australia, Capricorn and Genesis, 2 of the preeminent gold miners there as well as a number of producers and developers such as Vizsla and i-80 and Mansa. And that is a deliberate strategy over the course of the last 12 to 18 months of both diversifying our base, but also improving the quality of the underlying assets and the operators and counterparties who are operating those.

In terms of specific updates related to some of the assets in the quarter, the first 2 that I will talk to are Caserones and Karlawinda. Karlawinda, we had the mine expansion that has been going on over the past year. It was successfully commissioned on budget during the quarter. So towards the end of September, Capricorn announced they had completed that commissioning and we expect it now to be running at the higher approximately 150,000 ounce throughput rate. So about a 30% increase in attributable gold equivalent ounces to Elemental going forward. And you can see it's been pretty consistent in terms of the production historically.

They also earlier this year announced an increase in the resource and the reserves alongside that higher production rate that is going through. And so if you look at an asset like Karlawinda since we acquired it, it has, I think, a similar mine life at the time that we acquired it just on the reserves and not including the resources, which would approximately double it. We've benefited now from both the successful mine build, but also now an expansion of the mine that will deliver an increase in production at no cost to the company going forward and we have the optionality to the gold price.

And so it's a very good example of the embedded value that you have in royalties where we no longer have to contribute to the costs, but the operator, in this case, Capricorn are very successfully continuing to explore, add new ounces to the resources, convert existing resources into reserves and simultaneously expand the mine production so that it is now running at approximately 30% higher throughput rate. The second asset, Caserones. Caserones was the primary reason for lower-than-expected revenue in the quarter.

And you can see there the impact from Q2 to Q3, we had about $3.5 million lower revenue in Q3 than we did in the last quarter and that was due to a combination of storms in Chile. And the first one was very well publicized across the state and impacted a number of mines, particularly copper mines in Chile, and it was followed up by another storm that I think compounded the issues and delayed production in Q3. And I think they have announced since that they are updating their guidance down to the lower number for this year, but expecting production to recover both in Q4 and then getting back to normal.

So in terms of our royalty revenue, look, Caserones is a very long-life asset that in our view, we will be producing for many, many years to come. And I think that, that production there is really delayed and not lost to us in terms of revenue, but that was the primary cause of the lower revenue in the quarter of that -- that's all to Caserones. In terms of the next 3 assets, at Timok, we had again a very good quarter there continuing.

As mentioned previously, we did do a site visit to the mine earlier this year and got an update on both current mining, but also their exploration where they've been exceptionally active and the development of the Lower Zone, which is the major value driver at Timok and that is the porphyry that sits beneath the Upper Zone and they are actively developing and expect that we will have some updates from Zijin in the coming months and year on progress at the Lower Zone in addition to ongoing production from the Upper Zone, which is what we received now and then future plans at the MG Zone, which was the major discovery they made approximately 2 years ago within our royalty area as well.

So Timok remains an asset that will be paying us every year and we expect it to continue to appreciate in value as they get closer towards first production in the Lower Zone and then also looking at future plans for the MG Zone. At Bonikro, we have, again, very consistent quarter with Q2 and Q1 and the area where we received royalty from the pushback on Bonikro. That's expected to be a key part of the mine moving forward, and they are benefiting from some higher-grade areas there as well in the coming months and quarters. So we expect that to continue to perform at those levels for us.

And then lastly, in Leeville, this is Nevada Gold Mines in the U.S., and this is part of the Carlin complex. And we did have an update from the mine site team earlier this year as well for getting a better understanding of the mine plan going forward because our royalty covers part of the mine. And so it will sometimes fluctuate quarter-to-quarter. But again, really positive exploration results they have had in our royalty area in recent times and this is a mine that has been producing now for decades and will continue to do so in our opinion. So another really high-quality long-life asset operated by a Tier 1 miner.

In terms of some of our key development assets that we expect to come in, I think just touch on these 4 and 2 of them, in particular, the first 2, Panuco and Ruby Hill that have been added to the portfolio in the last 3 months with the completion of the Vizsla deal and the first close on Orion. So firstly, looking at Panuco in Mexico, and I think there are 2 real drivers there for the company. They are fully funded to build the mine. And the 2 key, I think, drivers for them are both planning on the security front and also on the permitting side.

And on the permitting side in Mexico, we have seen approximately 5 permits granted, 2 mining companies in Mexico since I think Q2 this year. And there's a couple that remain outstanding in Mexico and Vizsla is one of those in that list. So I think after a hiatus of 12 to 18 months with very little happening on the permitting front, there has been a steady stream of permits since Q2 this year, 4 different mining companies in Mexico getting those approved. So I think very positive on the permitting front in Mexico in general and specifically to Panuco, expect them to be on that list of projects awaiting that permit.

And then on the security front, I think waiting for the company to announce their plans on that side as well and getting back on site in a fulsome way and progressing the project. So those are the 2 key catalysts at Panuco. In terms of Ruby Hill, actually, the day after we announced the acquisition of this stream, there was a very good update out by the company, which was the first results from their drill program and they are doing a 130,000-meter drill program at Ruby Hill. So it's a really material exploration program partly funded by Franco-Nevada.

And they announced some very good results, a significant number above the existing resource grade, both up to, I think it was 3 kilometers to the east and 1 kilometer to the north. So material step-out drilling and results that a significant proportion were above the existing resource grade. So they're going to continue drilling that over the course of Q4 and going into Q1. And I think we expect an updated resource from them there in Q1. And I think very positive from the results that we have seen to date and they continue to aggressively to drill that out.

They also announced later in the quarter, they also announced that they are moving Ruby Hill forward in terms of their planning for projects in development. And Ruby Hill, just as a reminder, is the cornerstone asset for i-80 and they have announced that they are bringing it forward in the development time frame ahead of 2 of their other projects. And I think that was something we had anticipated, but it was good to see that announced formally by them and again, expect them to have further updates on that. But in summary, expanding the existing resource that we have coverage on there, at the same time as looking to bring the project forward as Elemental asset.

So 2 really material positive impact at that asset in the 6 or 7 weeks since we have that onstream. In terms of Laverton, we had a royalty -- existing royalty on part of the ground here and we picked up a larger royalty covering more of the ground another 2% in September of 2025. The operator, Genesis have been very active on the M&A front, consolidating ground in the Laverton District as well as surrounding. And this is one of the 2 primary hubs for Genesis in Laverton. So they have an existing mill there and they are going to come up with an updated mine plan that will look to optimize production from different ore sources.

And we expect that underground our royalty covers will be fed into that mine plan. And just to give you an idea, one of the key deposits at Beasley Creek that they call the first cab off the rank. It is double the grade of what they currently mine. We have about -- we have a 4% royalty on it. It is permitted, licensed about 20 kilometers on the road from the mill. And we expect that will be one of the first deposits that goes into their updated mine plan when they put it out.

So that would be very material for us and there are a number of deposits that we cover, our royalty covers there that we expect to come into the mine plan as they progress. Genesis have also said that they are looking to expand the mill that they have already operating there to enable them to process more given the M&A and given the consolidation they have done and the increased portfolio resources that they have.

So I think we should have some really material updates on that asset in Australia and Genesis have called it some of that ground as the best exploration ground that they have in West Australia, which is for a very well-known Australian gold producer and a management team who have historically been pretty conservative in what they said. I think that's a very positive indicator of what they see the potential there. And for us, as a royalty holder, look, you would not be able to buy that royalty from an existing operator or producer in that region now. Just to remind people, they're neighbors with Gold Fields and AngloGold Ashanti in that Laverton belt.

So I think the transaction we did, which was a third-party existing royalty that has been around for over a decade in September of last year. Big Sky Industrial (BSIN) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Carbon hub construction stays on schedule, with helium sales already locked in.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Big Sky Industrial Inc. (BSIN -3.44%) is transitioning from a small-cap exploration and production firm to an integrated industrial gas and carbon management company. Management reported that Phase 1 construction at the Big Sky Carbon Hub remains on schedule for first production in spring 2027. The company stated that the field side of the project is complete, including gathering lines and operational injection wells. Management indicated that its strategy involves leveraging long-term, take-or-pay helium contracts and the monetization of Section 45Q carbon sequestration tax credits. The company stated that its existing infrastructure and acreage already support future expansion beyond the initial processing facility's capacity.

Operator: Greetings, and welcome to the Big Sky Industrial, Inc.'s Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. I will now turn the conference over to Mason McGuire, VP of Finance and Strategy.

Mason McGuire: Good morning, and welcome to Big Sky Industrial, Inc. Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] Today's call is being recorded, and a replay will be available on the Investor Relations section of the company's website at bigskyindustrialinc.com. Before we begin, I would like to remind everyone that today's discussion will include forward-looking statements within the meaning of the federal securities laws. These statements are based on management's current expectations and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to materially differ. Please refer to the company's most recent SEC filings, including the Form 10-Q filed yesterday and the Form 10-K for a discussion of these risks.

Statements made on this call speak only as of today, and the company undertakes no obligation to update them. Joining us this morning are Ryan Smith, President and Chief Executive Officer; and Mark Zajac, Chief Financial Officer. I'll now turn the call over to Mr. Smith.

Ryan Smith: Thanks, Mason, and good morning, everyone. The third quarter was about execution. When we spoke in August, I said every structural piece of Phase 1 was in place, engineering, permitting, EPC, funding and offtake, and that what was left between here and first revenue was execution. That is where this team has spent the last 90 days in Houston and in Montana, and we are pleased with where the project stands. The field side of Phase 1 is now built. Our infield gathering and injection lines are complete. Our long lead equipment has been procured, including the power generation that will power the plant. Fabrication of the processing facility is underway, and construction remains on schedule.

We are targeting first production in spring 2027, and our helium is sold from day 1. Here's how I'll walk through the call this morning. Construction and operations first, then a direct update on our MRV application, then our commercial position and the market we're selling into. Mark will take you through the quarter and the capital structure, and I'll come back at the end with what's ahead. I'm going to start in the field because that's where the progress this quarter is most visible. Our infield gathering and injection lines are complete. Those are the lines that bring gas from our production wells to the processing facility and carry CO2 from the plant to the Big Rose sequestration asset.

The production wells are drilled, completed, and ready to tie in, and our Class II injection wells are operational. In other words, the field side of Phase 1 -- gas in, helium purified and sold, and CO2 captured -- is in place and ready for the plant. On the plant, our long lead equipment has been procured, including company-owned power generation. In August, we identified the generators as the longest lead time item on the project. They are purchased, on site, and owning our generation puts plant uptime in our own hands from day 1 while significantly improving our operational costs going forward. Fabrication of the modular processing facility is underway with our EPC partner.

The modular design is a big reason we remain comfortable and on schedule. Most of the build happens in the controlled fabrication environment rather than in the field, and far less can go sideways on site. On-site civil work begins in the coming weeks, and equipment installation runs from late in the fourth quarter into the first quarter of next year. We're targeting first production in spring 2027, and I want to be clear why we framed it as a window. It leaves sensible room for commissioning and for Montana winter, and I'd rather give a window than a specific date.

At initial operations, the plant is designed to produce greater than 14 million cubic feet of high-purity helium and to capture approximately 125,000 metric tons of CO2 a year. Now the MRV, as I know it's on the front of the mind of many of you who have dialed in today, our Big Rose MRV is in the final stage of the EPA's approval process. Big Rose is where we will sequester the CO2 captured in Phase 1 and in future phases. We submitted the plan on October 9, 2025. And because of the federal government shutdown, the agency accepted it on November 12, 2025.

We have addressed the EPA's comments and are awaiting final agency action, and nothing in the agency's feedback has changed our expectation of approval. While the process has taken longer than we optimistically hoped, historically, EPA approvals of comparable plans have generally taken 12 months or longer. So our timing is in line with precedent even if we had hoped for a quicker outcome. The review is detailed by design. It covers how CO2 will be monitored, measured and accounted for, including leak detection and verification of long-term sequestration, and the timing of the final decision rest with the agency.

Our responsibility to this point has been to respond promptly and keep the project moving forward, and that's exactly what we have done. The MRV approval is not a condition to starting Phase 1 plant or to selling helium. Our injection wells are permitted and operational, so we can sequester CO2 from the first day the plant runs. The approval timing does not change our resource base, our plant sequestration capacity or our helium offtake terms. Its impact is when we start generating and monetizing 45Q credits, not on the underlying economics of the project. We are advancing our credit monetization work in parallel with the EPA review so that we're positioned to move forward once approval is received.

Our second plan for Cut Bank was submitted on December 17, 2025. It supports CO2-enhanced oil recovery at our Cut Bank oil field over the longer term as we move through future phases, and it continues to move positively through the EPA process. Now to the commercial side of the platform. Our helium is sold from day 1. The offtake we signed in April is a 5-year, 100% take-or-pay contract with an investment-grade global industrial gas company, 1.2 million cubic feet a month at a fixed plant gate price of $285 per Mcf with CPI escalation beginning March 1, 2028, and the pricing redetermination in year 3.

The buyer handles all transportation, logistics and delivery, so we realize the full $285 with no deductions. Volume risk and demand risk sit with an investment-grade counterparty, not with us. The market we're selling into continues to make our case for us. On October 23, the Department of Energy designated helium a critical material under the Energy Act of 2020, citing its essential role in semiconductor manufacturing and growing supply chain risk. We have said for some time that an American producer of a strategically important industrial gas matters a great deal right now, and that designation puts it in writing. There is no substitute for helium in semiconductors, medical imaging, fiber optics or aerospace.

Domestic supply remains thin, and our redetermination gives us a way to participate in a stronger market. Cut Bank Oil Field keeps doing its job by providing low-decline oil production that supports the platform while we build and the captive CO2 outlet for enhanced oil recovery in future phases. With that, let me hand it to Mark to walk through the third quarter results and the capital structure.

Mark Zajac: Thanks, Ryan, and good morning, everyone. I'll cover 3 things this morning: the quarter's results, including the remediation item and lease operating expense, our liquidity and the financing work Ryan referenced. The full detail is in yesterday's press release and in our 10-Q. Starting with the quarter, revenue was $2 million, up 12% from $1.7 million a year ago as a 30% increase in our average realized price to $63.83 per BOE more than offset lower volumes following our 2025 divestiture. Production was 30,628 BOE, 76% oil. Lease operating expense was $2.1 million compared to $1 million a year ago.

The increase reflects approximately $1.2 million of remediation and related costs from the previously disclosed release of produced fluids from a gathering line in our legacy oil operations, which was identified and remediated during the quarter. We submitted a claim under our general liability policy, which provides $1 million of coverage for pollution cleanup costs after a $100,000 deductible, and we expect to recover up to $1 million of these costs. Because the claim is pending approval, no receivable has been recorded at the quarter end. Excluding these costs, lease operating expense was $0.9 million or $29.71 per BOE, essentially flat with the $29.58 a year ago.

Cash general and administrative expense was $1.6 million, down from $1.7 million a year ago and $1.8 million in the second quarter, primarily on lower accounting and tax professional fees. Adjusted EBITDA, which excludes the $1.2 million of remediation costs, was negative $0.8 million compared to a negative $1.3 million a year ago. Net loss was $3.5 million or $0.07 per share. On liquidity, we ended the quarter with $2.9 million of cash and $14.4 million of total liquidity, including $11.5 million of undrawn capacity under our senior secured credit facility. That facility carries a $20 million borrowing base, no financial covenant testing through the quarter ending March 31, 2027, and a May 2029 maturity.

On financing, we are running 2 complementary processes. The first is a new larger senior secured credit facility. Our existing facility was sized for the start of construction. As we approach first production with contracted helium revenue, we believe the asset supports more borrowing capacity, and a larger facility would expand the capacity and our financial flexibility. Those discussions are underway. The second is monetization of the Phase 1 Section 45Q credits. Under tax law, 45Q credits are transferable, which makes the Phase 1 credit stream a financeable asset in its own right and a potential source of nondilutive capital. Those discussions are progressing, and we will update the market as each process advances. With that, back to you, Ryan.

Ryan Smith: Thanks, Mark. Let me close with what's ahead because the next several months are full. Over the coming months, we have a sequence of milestones, each of which further derisks the project. On-site civil work starts in the coming weeks. Equipment installation runs from late in the fourth quarter into the first quarter, followed by commissioning. Our Big Rose MRV plan is in the final stage of the EPA's process, and our credit facility and 45Q monetization processes are advancing in parallel. First production comes in spring 2027 with our helium sold from day 1. Behind Phase 1 is Phase 2.

Engineering and planning are underway for a second larger plant on the same footprint using the same infrastructure, same permits and same field operations we are putting in place for Phase 1. Our acreage, our permitted wells and our geology already support 2 to 3x Phase 1 capacity with no new land required. We expect to make a final investment decision in the coming months following completion of financing and final plant sizing. Once Phase 1 is operating, Big Sky Industrial is no longer a small-cap E&P. We are an industrial gas producer with a contracted offtake, a carbon management business with policy-backed revenue and a low-decline oil business integrated as a captive CO2 outlet.

Small-cap E&P companies trade at roughly 3x EBITDA, midstream and gas processing at roughly 8x and blue-chip industrial gas companies at roughly 17x or higher. Those aren't our forecasts. They are public market multiples that anyone can verify. Our job is to keep executing the milestones and let the market award it. To put a fine point on the quarter, the field side of Phase 1 is complete, the long lead equipment is in hand, plant fabrication is underway and construction remains on schedule. Our helium is contracted from day 1, and the federal government has now formally recognized it as a critical material.

In August, I said the countdown to commercial operation was measured in months, not years, and there are fewer of them now. I want to thank our team in both Houston and Montana for their execution this quarter, and thank you to our shareholders for their continued support as we move from the build phase into the cash flow phase. The path to first production is clear, and we're executing against our plan. Operator, with that, please open the line for questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Our first question comes from Charles Meade with Johnson Rice.

Charles Meade: Good morning, Ryan and Mark, and to the rest of the Big Sky team there. Ryan, thank you for the detail really in the context of that 12-month context for the EPA approval. I think I was aware of that, but I hadn't really focused on it. But I wondered if you could just add a little bit more color and tell us how many rounds of comments you've had from the EPA that you've responded to? And what was the last -- what was -- when did your last round of comments come? How long ago?

Ryan Smith: Yes. Good question. Some of this I probably can't get too granular on. But from a high level, I think the expectation going into this was very similar. I'll appeal to the finance crowd a little bit, but very similar to an S-1 with the SEC, where you submit your project, your proposal on the front end, you get reviewed, the review starts. And then over the allotted expected time period going forward, you get questions, comments that you respond to. And in an ideal scenario, those questions and comments get less and less and less as you move forward.

Our process has mimicked that 100% to where the first round we got, I believe it was the very end of last year, maybe early this year, was the most thorough and then a few back and forth turns in the 4 to 5 comment range that got materially less as we move forward is the blueprint that we expected and that occurred with us. So our last round of comments -- again, this is a ballpark timeline -- was around Labor Day, which internally, our opinion was those comments were extremely light, and we instantly responded to those.

And since then, in the company's opinion, our review has been complete, and nothing in the feedback that we've gotten has reflected otherwise. So it's been -- to answer your question, it's been, call it, 6-plus weeks since that last turn occurred.

Charles Meade: Got it. That's good detail, Ryan. And then on the construction of your plant, I wondered if you could share a few of the items on the critical path to startup that you and the Board are the most focused on.

Ryan Smith: Yes. I mean, I think a high level, right, I'll give you a layup answer. It's really everything, right, just because everything ties into it coming online when we want it to come online. A lot of the long lead time items, and I would say, like, the riskiest items were us getting in the queue for generators, membranes, et cetera, pumps, pipe materials that we've gotten ahead of. So right now, fabrication of the plant is beginning off-site. Civil work is beginning on site. And that's complicated, but on the risk spectrum, this is a very, like, old proven type of processing technology, and clearing a site, et cetera, is pretty simple.

So a lot of the riskiest items are behind us. Just going forward, making sure that all of these fabrication assembling the pieces off-site, moving it on. Unfortunately, weather does play a part in moving it on leads to this spring date. The gathering system was a very important thing for us, obviously, so we can move this gas. You want to have that gathering system done by end of November just because you don't want to be operating up there in the winter for installing a gathering system. That's been completed. So again, every single part, we're extremely focused on.

But right now, it's really just the execution of putting the materials together and making sure that's being done on a timely basis going forward.

Operator: Our next question is from Tom Kerr with Zacks SCR.

Thomas Kerr: Any update on the CapEx numbers for the industrial gas build-out? We're at $15 million in 2026. I guess we have about 3 quarters left of build-out cost. What do those numbers look like? Have those changed? And then related to that, is there a maintenance CapEx number once everything gets built out for the industrial gas properties?

Ryan Smith: So I think we have probably, I'd say, 2 quarters left of spend. I would stick to the number we gave on our Q just because intra-quarter numbers are always weird numbers to give. We have spent a little bit this quarter. So I would add a couple of million dollars to the 3Q 9/30 number. But that's all moving along.

I think the only increase that we've had materially that wasn't originally in the plan is what we announced earlier on us acquiring power generation, which net-net is an upfront capital spend, but over the life cycle of operational costs, you more than make your money back and then some and also aren't exposed to increasing power prices in perpetuity, in my opinion, going forward. And on the maintenance costs, the maintenance costs on the plant are not very significant. Of course, there will be some. It's built into the numbers that we've put out in our investor presentation. So you -- of course, like with any machine, you'll have some maintenance costs.

But that's built into our forecast, our operating expenses and outside of something that's major and unforeseen, it's an extremely low maintenance cost on the plant going forward once it's up and running.

Thomas Kerr: Got it. That makes sense. One more quick one. On the 45Q monetization, are you able to provide any updates or further color on the discussions? Are you talking about finance companies or energy companies? Are you talking about a big chunk of the $130 million, a small chunk? Just I know you may not be able to discuss it, but any further color on that?

Ryan Smith: Yes. I mean, again, I'll reiterate what you said. There's only so much you can say. But from a high level, and this is reflected in the call and the earnings release, we're in talks with multiple parties ranging from, I would say, the largest financial institutions and then overall, the biggest pool of, I'll call it, credit finance parties, which comes in multiple different avenues, are big insurance, big tech, big financial institutions, large entities that have very significant tax burdens forecasted in the perpetuity in the future. And I would say all of our conversations reflect that group.

Every group has a different structure and a different need and a different desire on what they transact on going from annual all the way to the entire pool. Some entities want to do more of a financing structure than an outright purchase. I Saw the Future of Robotics in a Virtual Duck That Chases My Cat

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Allow me to introduce you to the robot cat butler I’m developing with help from AI.

Using OpenAI’s GPT-6 Sol, I taught a virtual version of Microduck—a cute robot from Pollen Robotics and Hugging Face—to walk around a room and identify any bengal cats in its vicinity. (The clip above shows the robot waddling toward a target feline; its eye view is shown top right.)

I’m still waiting patiently for a physical Microduck—please hurry up and ship it, guys—but simulated behavior is relatively simple, so it should transfer over to the real thing. I plan to have the robot automatically dispense a treat for my pet bengal, Leona, perhaps by bowing its head to release one from a 3D-printed, head-mounted storage chamber.

I was able to code and train the virtual robot in just a few hours, which is, quite frankly, incredible. Previously, this kind of thing might have taken me weeks to figure out. It was possible because OpenAI’s latest models are remarkably good at understanding and manipulating 3D representations of the physical world and then translating that into useful code, agentic actions, and robot training.

Real roboticists are using the latest models to do some amazing stuff. When OpenAI released Astra this past September, University of California, Berkeley professor Ken Goldberg and his students saw demos that showcased its ability to render 3D images. They wondered if it could help with the simulations required to train different kinds of robots. These simulations need to be incredibly detailed and extremely accurate to ensure that sophisticated behavior—a humanoid that can dance or a robot hand that can grasp—will transfer faithfully to the real world.

One member of Goldberg’s lab gave Astra access to an Nvidia tool called Newton, showed it a video clip of a real robot in action, and asked if it could create a simulation of the scene.

“What blew us all away was that within an hour it had produced a full 3D simulation with all the parameters set—the position of the camera, the lighting, the background, all the materials,” Goldberg tells me.

Astra is also able to automate much of the drudgery involved with training a robot in simulation, Goldberg says, by running experiments and examining the results.

This is precisely what I found while training the virtual Microduck. Along with GPT-6 Sol, I used an agentic harness from Nous Research called Hermes and a Nvidia DGX Spark, a personal computer that contains a powerful AI chip.

I’ve previously written about using agents and coding models to configure, program, and debug physical robots. Normally, this kind of work requires significant expertise; modern coding models make it a cinch. But OpenAI’s newest model went far beyond just writing code.

It used a piece of Nvidia software called Isaac Sim to feed instructions to a model that controlled the virtual robot, watch videos of it in action to assess how well things were working, and suggest tweaks to its training regime.

This process allowed me to train the Microduck to walk around without toppling over. I initially tried getting GPT-6 Sol to teach the robot to moonwalk—I fed it a GIF of Michael Jackson sliding across the stage, which it tried to copy—but after hours of effort, it only resulted in a very jaunty backward jog. (The robots don’t have us totally beat quite yet.)

Jim Fan, a researcher at Nvidia who has collaborated with Goldberg, says the way AI can now control robots mirrors how software agents function. LLMS can use models trained to control physical actions—visual language action (VLA) models—just as they access different software on a computer.

This week, Fan, Goldberg, and others open sourced an agentic framework for robots called Aspire, which allows a robot to discover and use new skills. One example: GPT-6 Astra figured out how to use a pair of robot arms to solve a Rubik’s cube.

“It picks it up, tries to twist it, but has the wrong angle,” Fan says. “Then it reasons for a very, very long time, but it eventually is able to solve it because it learns the skills over time.”

At the Conference on Robot Learning (CoRL), held in Austin, Texas, this week, Fan gave a keynote speech in which he talked about the incredible realization that AI models can automate a lot of the work needed to have a robot do sophisticated things. Fan showed off a demo in which Astra was put in control of a robot arm and told to paint a picture of the Golden Gate Bridge. Using some paint and brushes nearby, it duly completed the task.

Of course, getting robots to work reliably and efficiently is still extremely difficult, requiring significant engineering expertise. The vision system on my robot duck does not work well enough to be deployed in a real product.

Still, the ease and speed of training robots with AI could soon have an enormous impact on manufacturing and e-commerce, and Goldberg says a number of industrial robotics and automation firms have shown an interest in the approach.

Much like vibe coding a few years ago, we may well see breakthroughs in “vibe robotics” as hobbyists and engineers realize how easy it is to program and train robots. And, assuming I can perfect my robot helper, Leona may never have to go without treats again.

This is an edition of Will Knight’s AI Lab newsletter. Read previous newsletters here.

Google brings the Gemini app to Apple CarPlay

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The chatbot was already available through the Google Maps app.

Continuing on its apparent quest to ensure the chatbot is inescapable on as many screens, speakers and experiences as possible, Google has brought the Gemini app to Apple CarPlay. It's available starting today for all supported Gemini Live languages.

Google previously made the AI assistant available on CarPlay through the Google Maps app. Now, you'll be able to access it just by tapping the Gemini icon on your display. Google said you can pick up where you left off with Gemini in your car or start a new conversation with the chatbot.

The company suggested drivers could use Gemini to prep for a meeting during their commute, catch up on emails, hear their Daily Brief or get recommendations for local places worth checking out in an area. Your conversations will sync with the Gemini iPhone app and remain in your chat history.

The Gemini app is now available directly on Apple CarPlay. iOS users can have free-flowing conversations with Gemini Live right from their car's display, hands-free.

Use Gemini in CarPlay to catch-up on what you have coming up in your schedule, talk through your inbox, or... pic.twitter.com/1s4luZ6q0v

To use the assistant on CarPlay, you'll need to make sure you have the latest version of the Gemini app on your iPhone and the microphone permissions are on. You'll need to have used Gemini Live at least once on your iPhone as well.

Google brought the Gemini app to CarPlay not long after it enabled Personal Assistance (which can access details from other Google apps for more personalized responses) in the chatbot on Android Auto. The company is bringing Personal Intelligence to Gemini on cars with Google built-in as well.

CoreWeave turns to customer prepayments, shorter contracts to fund AI growth

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CoreWeave is rewriting how it pays for the AI boom, according to the company’s third-quarter earnings, reported on Tuesday. CoreWeave, a neocloud that rents out the computing power behind AI, said it’s increasingly asking customers to pay up front, borrowing against specific contracts, and even pursuing short-term deals that could lead to higher prices.

The new strategies could help CoreWeave finance its growth more efficiently while squeezing more cash from existing infrastructure. But these bets only hold if today’s demand and pricing power continue.

CoreWeave reported $3.66 billion in revenue, up 168% from the year prior, but it also posted a $709 million net loss and $846 million in quarterly interest expense.

“We will not use the unsecured debt market to fund our business at current yields, nor do we need to,” CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator said during Tuesday’s earnings call.

Instead, CoreWeave’s plan is to rely on loans backed by specific customer contracts, drawing on funds as needed to finance most of infrastructure it still has to build to deliver on its significant backlog of $132.5 billion.

CoreWeave also said it expects about $9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits over the next three months, including more than $3 billion tied to signing new customer contracts for capacity it has already built, which requires no additional spending on hardware.

Rivals Nebius and IREN have also relied on hefty Microsoft prepayments to pay for computing capacity, signaling that the industry is moving toward asking Big Tech to pay up for their buildouts.

Upfront payments could alleviate CoreWeave’s immediate borrowing needs, but they also commit the company to providing services at a price that might be less attractive down the road if compute demand continues to surge.

CoreWeave is also looking to capitalize on that growing demand by signing more shorter-term contracts that can command much higher prices.

The company raised prices across its offerings by about 25% in July and by another 10% later in the quarter. Prices are still climbing this quarter, in a way that “more than offset[s] cost increases,” said Intrator.

“We are also allocating more capacity to short-term opportunities, where the pricing justifies it, selling capacity at more than $40 million per megawatt annually, while pricing for new infrastructure on four and five-year contracts is moving to $20 million to $25 million per megawatt,” the chief executive said.

CoreWeave’s financing strategy now revolves around those opportunities. In August, the company closed a $2.6 billion loan, its first designed specifically for shorter-term customer contracts. Known as DDTL 5.5, it lets CoreWeave borrow in installments as it needs the money.

While short-term deals let CoreWeave charge higher prices and change them more frequently, it’s a tradeoff. Lenders give more weight to longer-term contracts, even if they’re priced lower, because they value predictable cash flow and consistency to repay the loan above all.

Even though Wall Street reacted positively to CoreWeave’s earnings, bond investors are much less sanguine. Last month, yields on some of the company’s bonds hit above 15%, highlighting that investors are concerned about the debt needed to fund data center buildouts before those investments see a return.

In short, CoreWeave is finding new ways to expand without relying on unsecured debt. The strategy it has chosen could boost profits, but it also carries greater risk around contract renewals and pricing — and it just goes to show how dependent the AI infrastructure boom is on sustained, and as yet unproven, demand.

TechCrunch has reached out to CoreWeave for more information about its loan facility.

Argá Medtech Completes Enrollment of Paroxysmal AF Cohort in the COHERENT-AF Pivotal IDE Study

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Prospective, multicenter study evaluates the Coherent Sine Burst Electroporation Ablation Platform, a sine wave-based pulsed field ablation technology, in drug-refractory atrial fibrillation; persistent AF cohort enrollment continues

SAN DIEGO, Nov. 11, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- ArgÃ¡ Medtech, a medical technology company developing sine wave-based pulsed field ablation (PFA) for cardiac arrhythmias, today announced the completion of enrollment in the paroxysmal atrial fibrillation (PAF) cohort of COHERENT-AF, its pivotal U.S. Investigational Device Exemption (IDE) study of the Coherent Sine Burst Electroporation (CSE) Platform. The PAF cohort enrolled 150 intention-to-treat subjects across 27 sites in Europe and the United States. Enrollment in the persistent AF (PersAF) cohort is ongoing and expected to complete in the next two months.

"Completing enrollment in the paroxysmal AF cohort is an important milestone for COHERENT-AF," said Emile Daoud, MD, Director of Electrophysiology Research; Hatton Research Institute at Bethesda North Hospital, Cincinnati, Ohio and the leading enroller in COHERENT-AF. "In our procedures, the CSE Platform has consistently delivered pulmonary vein isolation with a streamlined, single-catheter workflow, and the ability to address the pulmonary veins, posterior wall and cavo-tricuspid isthmus without exchanging catheters has been a meaningful advantage in our lab. We look forward to the completion of the persistent AF cohort and the 12-month effectiveness results."

COHERENT-AF (ClinicalTrials.gov: NCT06784466) is a prospective, multicenter, non-randomized pre-market study evaluating the safety and effectiveness of the CSE Platform in adults with symptomatic, drug-refractory PAF or PersAF. The study plan includes enrollment of up to 410 subjects in the United States and Europe, including 150 intention-to-treat subjects in each of the PAF and PersAF cohorts. Additional subjects will be enrolled in a second phase of the IDE investigation.

All subjects undergo pulmonary vein isolation (PVI) with the CSE system. Posterior wall and other left atrial substrate and cavo-tricuspid isthmus ablation may be performed with the CSE system at the operator's discretion. Each cohort is evaluated separately against prespecified performance goals for safety and effectiveness, with subjects followed through 12 months.

"Reaching full enrollment so quickly in the paroxysmal AF cohort reflects the commitment of our investigators and study teams," said David Neale CEO, ArgÃ¡ Medtech. "We are now focused on completing the persistent AF cohort and generating the evidence needed to support FDA and CE-Mark regulatory submissions of the CSE Platform."

About the Coherent Sine Burst Electroporation Platform

The ArgÃ¡ Medtech CSE Platform features a single ablation catheter that can be configured by a simple stylet exchange to deliver circular, linear and focal ablation lesions. The CSE generator delivers controlled bursts of microsecond sine-waved based pulsed field ablation (PFA) across two, four or eight electrodes and can be titrated to treat the full spectrum of atrial tissue from thin-walled RV to thick septal or intramural circuits.

CAUTION: Investigational device. Limited by Federal (or United States) law to investigational use.

ArgÃ¡ Medtech SA is a venture capital-backed medical technology company dedicated to developing and delivering innovative technology to help patients affected by heart rhythm disorders. Its PFA platform uses sine waves to deliver energy and includes the SineBurstâ¢ Ablation Catheter and the SineWaveâ¢ Ablation Generator. The company is based at BiopÃ´le Lausanne in Epalinges, Switzerland, with U.S. operations based in San Diego, California. Investors include Advent Life Sciences, Earlybird, Gilde Healthcare, High-Tech GrÃ¼nderfonds and MedFocus. For more information, visit argamedtech.com.

Nike’s Goal With the Structure Plus? Make Stability Less Boring

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The brand’s classic stability shoe gets the super trainer treatment with a tall stack height, lighter weight, and PEBA added to the midsole.

We earn a commission for products purchased through some links in this article. Why Trust Us?

How do you change runners’ conception about stability? Last December at The Running Event in San Antonio, Texas, Nike Expert PLM Ashley Campbell held up the answer: the Structure Plus.

“Stability can be boring,” she admitted. The Structure Plus makes it exciting.

The shoe follows the Vomero Plus, a super trainer version of the Vomero 18. The Structure Plus is the super-ified version of the Structure 26. Where the latter is more accessible for $140, the “plus” iteration is the original amplified in every aspect.

The Structure Plus became my chosen shoe for long runs during my 2026 Boston Marathon training cycle. Here’s how the shoe won me over on a 12-miler, and why you should consider trying a stability super trainer.

While developing the Structure Plus, Campbell said the team set out to make the super trainer “more supportive, smoother, softer, and more palatable.”

Tech-wise, you can see how Nike landed on the first three. The shoe’s platform is wider to provide support. A convex flat shape of sidewall provides stability, the density underneath the arch acting as a stabilizer to help with alignment. A dual layer of foams, like in the Vomero Plus, provides a soft, energizing ride.

In terms of making the shoe more palatable, Campbell wanted the Structure Plus to be all-inclusive. “You don’t have to be an overpronator [to run in the shoe.]”

At 42 mm in the heel and 32 mm in the forefoot, it’d make sense if you expected the shoe to be heavy and unstable. But the Structure Plus challenges these notions.

At 10.9 ounces, men’s 10, the shoe is lighter than the Structure 26, which weighs 11.3 ounces. The maximal cushioning is a layer of PEBA-based ZoomX, a soft foam that delivers high rebound (the foam is also in Nike’s premium marathon shoe, the Alphafly), and TPE-based ReactX, a firmer foam that helps stabilize your ride (the same foam found in the Vomero 18).

Despite the high stack height and max cushioning and stability components, the shoe didn’t weigh me down on my runs, nor did it cause my feet to roll. I didn’t feel like I was forced into alignment. The shoes guide you instead of directing your foot.

It was such a contrast after running in the Vomero Plus the weekend before. During those miles, it got to the point where my friends were getting concerned after I tripped over myself for the umpteenth time. My next long run was smooth, all thanks to the Structure Plus for keeping me stable.

I’m a neutral runner, but as the miles pile on, like they do on long runs, my form gets erratic due to fatigue. It’s those later miles where the Structure Plus saves me from hitting the pavement, or worse, benching myself with injury.

Concerning actual overpronators, our testers were satisfied with the support the shoe provided.

“What I liked most about the Nike Structure Plus was the strong sense of support it delivered on every run,” said tester and overpronator Julian Caiazzo. “The shoe felt stable and dependable without ever coming across as stiff or restrictive. It gave me the confidence to settle into my stride, and even with all that structure, the ride still felt smooth and enjoyable.”

Another overpronator, Katherine Thomas, admitted she usually avoids Nike shoes because of their lack of stability. She was pleasantly surprised with the support of the Structure Plus.

If there’s any reason why you should try the Structure Plus, it isn’t just that it’ll change your preconceived notion of a stability shoe; it’ll also change what you think of Nike trainers.

Like Thomas, I also avoided Nike shoes (unless I had to test them) because of their lack of stability and narrow fit. The Structure Plus doesn’t feel like a Nike shoe; there’s no squeezing on the sides of my midfoot, no pinching by my toes. And yet, I reap that quintessential Nike ride, that layer of ZoomX adding more pop to my strides. Those long runs are anything but boring slogs. I can emphatically say that Nike achieved its goal of making stability—and my runs—more exciting.

Since 2013, she has consecutively run the Boston Marathon. She also has a master’s degree in gastronomy from Boston University and was formerly a professional baker for two years before hanging up her apron.

"It's just wild the talent that they let go"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Nothing zaps the joy right out of launch day celebrations like getting laid off alongside half of your team, but that's exactly what happened to dozens of Id Software developers during Microsoft's July 2026 bloodbath (not to be confused with its September 2026 bloodbath) alongside the release of the Revelations DLC for Doom: The Dark Ages. Amid reports that the studio was gutted down to support studio size, the total confirmed number of laid off employees, per Game Developer, was 136 as of this past July, which current studio leadership has confirmed was about half of the team. That's an absolutely devastating loss of institutional talent, but a group of former Id Software devs has risen from the ashes as new worker-owned studio Hidden Horses. In the studio's announcement documentary, they don't hold back.

"It's just wild the talent that they let go. It's just wild that they would attack Id like that the day before we release our DLC. It really felt like an attack," says Hidden Horses co-founder and former Id Software tools programmer John-Michael Quinlan.

"It was probably emotionally the worst day of my life," says former Id Software senior technical designer Jordan Turner, who's now co-founder and, fittingly, technical designer at Hidden Horses. "I don't think I've cried that much in my entire life."

"I did not see it coming at the time that I was told," says former Id Software programmer, now Hidden Horses rendering programmer, Regan Carver. "I just told my wife, 'well that was not a fun conversation.' She thought I was joking at first, and then she realized I wasn't. I was in shock, but she was in tears.

"I usually spend the release days scouring social media looking for all the good news. It drives up my morale ... I'm always excited to see people receiving our stuff well and knowing I was a small part of it, so that was gone."

Amid a period of historic tumult in the gaming industry, it's easy to become desensitized by news of another mass layoff, but hearing from the workers themselves in this way really underscores the immense human toll of corporate downsizing.

Former Id Software AI engineering lead Daniel Young says the hardest part about being laid off was losing friends he'd worked with for years. "It hit me pretty hard. I think that was the biggest thing was the people that I was gonna be leaving behind. I hate to say it's like a death in the family, but it's almost like that's what it felt like," says Young.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

"Personally the biggest thing was that I'm trying to help my kids through college," says Todd Boyce, former Id Software lead VFX artist and Hidden Horses technical artist. "I have two kids in college right now, and that is really expensive. That's probably the thing that hurts me the most is the possibility of them being financially stressed because of something that wasn't even in their control, and for a layoff that honestly didn't need to happen."

Hidden Horses is a small studio of 19 developers according to this announcement video, and its Kickstarter describes its debut title, Danger Pay, as "a single player FPS based around a deadly gameshow with high-octane action and gameplay focused design." Meanwhile, Hidden Horses producer Andrew Willis says players can expect "absolutely stellar out-of-this-world fun gameplay, amazing and super cohesive and stylistic visuals, and really optimized gameplay on the technical side."

Technical designer Tim Locke says one key difference between working at a corporate-owned studio like Id and now Hidden Horses is creative freedom. "Being able to do what we want and talk through, like, what are our ideas, like, which ones resonate with the most people and how do we move forward with that. That's really the difference, and it has been different so far. It's been great."

Echoing that sentiment, Hidden Horses lighting artist Tyler DeLisle says "the people that were let go at Id ... we were all, none of us were slackers. We were all best of the best, top of our game, so now that we're all given this opportunity to come together and keep doing what we love to do, but kind of untethered and more collaborative, I think we can really put out something special."

"If even a small measure of those games that we've worked on in the industry, for me over the past 20 years, is able to be distilled down into a game for our fans, I think they're gonna get something really special," says Quinlan. "And I think you only have to look at the pedigree of the people involved to know that we can do something really special here."

Inside Xbox, "the only concrete thing they have communicated is they want higher profits, and they want it now," says id Software employee

Sen. Warren, Merkley raise ethics concerns over Trump aide’s prediction market bets

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

In August, the CFTC ordered Gabriel Perez to pay more than $172,000 after he allegedly read Trump's prepared remarks shortly before delivery and placed his bets on Kalshi mention markets.

Democratic Sens. Elizabeth Warren and Jeff Merkley are demanding answers from the White House after its teleprompter operator was accused of using advance information to place bets on President Donald Trump's speeches.

The lawmakers say that the White House has failed to take "meaningful action to address ethics concerns," in a letter to White House Counsel Will Scharf.

"We are writing to request information about how the White House is responding to allegations from earlier this year that a key presidential aide enriched himself by abusing access to nonpublic information, and how this alleged incident is shaped by the White House’s lax ethics and anticorruption policies and practices," they said.

In August, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission ordered Gabriel Perez to pay more than $172,000 after he allegedly read Trump's prepared remarks shortly before delivery and placed his bets on Kalshi mention markets, which try to predict specific words that public figures will use. Perez's situation is one of several in which people have used inside information to place bets on prediction markets, including an active-duty U.S. Army soldier who used confidential information to place bets on Polymarket ahead of former Venezuelan President Nicolás Maduro’s capture earlier this year.

Warren is set to take on a larger role after Democrats took control of the Senate and House in last week's midterm elections. She will lead the Senate Banking Committee, where she has said she would seek to "end the current Wild West-style lack of oversight in this area, with corrupt insiders profiting." Another Democrat, Rep. Ro Khanna, who is weighing a 2028 presidential bid, called for a ban on sports prediction markets on Tuesday.

In the letter, Warren and Merkley said that the White House needs to do more than send a warning memo and take heed of what other administrations have done, including mandatory training.

"More action is required—from the President, from you, and from other White House officials—to provide the public with confidence in the integrity of the federal government under President Trump," the senators said.

Specifically, Warren and Merkley said they want information on whether other employees had "misappropriated insider knowledge to place any bets, trades, purchase contracts, or taken any similar action for personal gain," and if training is available for nonpublic information.

The White House did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

Watch Out Hollywood: Creators and Sports Are Eating More of the Content Budgets

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new report from KPMG shines a lot on where the content spend is flowing, and traditional film and TV is losing out.

Where the entertainment business spends its content budget is shifting, with sports and independent creators reaping the rewards.

The consultancy KPMG released its annual media content spend analysis on Thursday, calculating the totals and outlining where the major entertainment platforms (like Disney and Netflix, but also YouTube, Meta and Amazon) are spending their money. Traditional film and TV content still has a role, but the report underscores how the winds are shifting.

The good news? Content spend is continuing to rise. In 2025, the companies KPMG tracks topped $207 billion, up from $156 billion in 2021. KPMG calculates spend based on costs associated with film and TV production, programming and licensing; sports rights; revenue sharing payments to creators; and music costs.

But while most traditional players (Paramount, Comcast, Disney) are seeing their content spend stagnate, it is companies like YouTube, Amazon and Meta where spend is rising.

That is reflected in the data, where YouTube is now king of the spenders, with an estimated $28 billion annually. Creator content now accounts for $46 billion of the annual content pie, up 75 percent from 2021. Sports meanwhile is now 29 percent, up 59 percent from 2021, as places like Netflix, Amazon, Apple and others pour cash into the space.

And of course places like Netflix, Disney+ and others are also cutting creator deals, bringing more types of content into their streaming fold.

“Creator content is taking on a larger role in the content portfolio because it can build frequent engagement, attract highly engaged audiences, and create new opportunities for advertising and monetization,” said Frank Albarella, Jr., U.S. sector leader, media and telecommunications, at KPMG US. “As the lines between creator-led and traditional entertainment blur, the question for media companies is how those formats work together to win consumer engagement and drive value.”

In 2021, film, TV and streaming content accounted for 71 percent of all content spend, but the new KPMG report finds that number sinking to 64 percent, as both sports and creator content surge.

“A successful ‘super app’ starts with a clear view of what your audience values,” said Scott Purdy, media sector leader, strategy, KPMG U.S. “The opportunity is to combine the content, experiences, and benefits that give your customers more reasons to stay, while making that relationship more valuable over time.”

Still, the data shows a shifting landscape for Hollywood, one that newly-enlarged giants like Fox-Roku and Skydance will need to navigate. Premium film and TV remains the heart of Hollywood, but it is careening toward a future where it is a smaller and smaller piece of the puzzle.

Israel Discount Bank Becomes First in Country to Offer Bitcoin and Ethereum Trading

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Discount Bank will launch direct Bitcoin and Ethereum trading through its mobile app.

Customers can trade crypto without opening accounts with external exchanges.

Discount Bank will waive account management fees for the first six months.

Israel Discount Bank, a $150 billion bank, has become the first in the country to offer Bitcoin and Ethereum trading through its mobile app. From November 26, customers can buy, hold, and sell both cryptocurrencies without using a separate exchange or wallet.

This puts the bank ahead of rival Bank Leumi, which plans to launch crypto trading in early 2027.

On November 11, Israel Discount Bank announced that its new crypto trading service will go live on November 26, making it the first Israeli bank to offer direct in-app buying and selling of digital assets.

The service will initially support Bitcoin and Ethereum.

Eligible private customers aged 18 and above can trade the two assets directly from their bank accounts, provided their accounts are enabled for securities activity and they meet the bank’s terms.

Users will not need to open an account with a foreign crypto exchange or manage a separate wallet. Bitcoin and Ethereum will be held through bank wallets using Fireblocks’ digital asset infrastructure, while proceeds from sales can return directly to the customer’s bank account.

Discount Bank CEO Avi Levi called the launch “a first step” toward building the bank’s digital currency infrastructure.

One of the key features is the treatment of crypto taxes. Discount Bank said customers will be able to offset capital gains and losses from digital-asset activity against other investments in their portfolio for withholding-tax purposes.

This keeps the tax calculation within the banking and investment account, rather than leaving customers to handle the process separately.

The bank will also waive account management fees for the first six months. However, trading commissions are reported at 2% per purchase or sale.

The launch could give Discount Bank an early lead as Israeli banks compete to attract customers interested in crypto.

Bank Leumi announced a similar plan in August, allowing customers to trade Bitcoin, Ethereum and Solana through its Leumi Trade app. However, the service is not expected to launch until early 2027, as the bank moves ahead with its partnership with Galaxy Digital.

This is not Leumi’s first attempt to enter crypto trading. In 2022, it planned to launch a similar service with Paxos, but the Bank of Israel blocked the move.

Reed's (REED) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Gross margin expanded and losses narrowed, but management says much work remains.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Reed's, Inc. (REED +15.08%) reported a year-over-year decrease in net sales while expanding gross margins through reduced promotional allowances and logistics optimization. Management prioritized stabilizing the balance sheet by refinancing existing debt and completing a ten million dollar private placement to support working capital and strategic growth initiatives. The company is focusing on a turnaround plan that includes expanding its retail footprint via a national broker network and developing new product lines for 2027. Management stated that operational improvements in delivery and handling costs have partially offset sales volume declines, and they expect sequential financial progress to continue through the end of the fiscal year.
